<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

## user

 What does ingest do? Tell me what ingest does.


## assistant

`ingest` takes source material you’ve saved and files useful information into your AI OS.

It handles three main inputs:

- **Session transcripts:** identifies the student, reads the transcript, and records what you taught, where the student struggled, assigned work, and follow-ups in their student folder.
- **Articles and web clips:** extracts takeaways, updates relevant wiki pages, and updates the index and log.
- **Bulk exports:** inventories files and date ranges first, then lets you choose what to process.

Before filing, it checks for duplicates. It preserves the original source and logs what changed.

The distinction: **`ingest` prepares your internal records. `lsat-session-lesson` creates the finished student-facing lesson and interactive preview.** Their transcript intake overlaps, but their outputs differ.

## user

Tell me the difference between each of these skills in simple language. And tell me which one of these are redundant. Which one of them are better or worse? Tell me what any of these they're doing.      [$ingest](C:\Users\Joshu\\.agents\skills\ingest\SKILL.md)


## assistant

| Skill | What it does |
|---|---|
| **`second-brain`** | Sets up a **new** knowledge base: folders, starter pages, and agent instructions. |
| **`second-brain-query`** | Answers questions using knowledge already saved in your wiki. |
| **`second-brain-ingest`** | Reads a new source and turns it into linked wiki pages: a source summary and relevant topic pages. |
| **`ingest`** | Finds incoming files, checks duplicates, and routes them. It also directly processes articles, student transcripts, and exports. |

**The real redundancy is between `ingest` and `second-brain-ingest`.** Both describe reading articles, discussing takeaways, updating wiki pages, and logging the result. `ingest` says it delegates that work to `second-brain-ingest`, but then includes its own article-processing procedure. That creates two competing instructions.

My recommendation:

- **Keep `ingest` as the front door.** It fits your workspace better: file discovery, duplicate checks, student mapping, and source preservation.
- **Keep `second-brain-ingest` as an internal wiki-writing procedure.** Its source summaries and linked topic pages are useful. Remove the duplicated article procedure from `ingest`.
- **Keep `second-brain-query` separate.** Reading existing knowledge is a different job from adding new knowledge.
- **Treat `second-brain` as setup-only.** You already have a vault, so it has little everyday value here.

`ingest` has the stronger operational safeguards. `second-brain-ingest` has more detailed wiki-writing instructions, but its claim that one source normally touches 10–15 pages encourages unnecessary page creation.

`second-brain` also has obvious template mistakes, including duplicated Codex options. It needs cleanup before being used to initialize another vault.

None of these creates the finished student-facing lesson. That belongs to **`lsat-session-lesson`**.

## developer

## user

 I don't get what /ingest does versus /second-brain-ingest. What's the difference? What's the difference between the two? I'm confused as to how... why there would need to be two skills and what's the...


## assistant

**You’re confused because they currently duplicate part of the same job.**

The intended distinction is:

- **`ingest`:** “Find this file, check whether we already processed it, and decide where it belongs.”
- **`second-brain-ingest`:** “Read this source and turn its useful information into wiki pages.”

For example, with an article, `ingest` should find it and check duplicates, then pass it to `second-brain-ingest` to update the wiki.

But `ingest` also contains instructions to update the wiki itself. That duplication makes the boundary unclear.

**You don’t need two separate skills to invoke.** My recommendation is one public skill, **`ingest`**, with the detailed wiki-writing procedure stored as an internal reference. You say “ingest this,” and it handles everything.

Your workflow is:

   I preserve the source, check duplicates, discuss takeaways, and integrate the useful knowledge.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
These AGENTS.md instructions replace all previously provided AGENTS.md instructions.

# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

--- project-doc ---

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

Unfortunately, it has now become clear that the magazine\'s publisher will not allow the managing editor to make the changes she has proposed, and without these changes, the magazine\'s circulation will not\\\\r\\\\nrise as quickly over the next ten years as it has over the last ten. So the magazine will not be the largest-selling martial arts magazine ten years from now.</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p>The argument\'s reasoning is flawed because the argument</p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"identifies some changes required for the magazine\'s circulation to continue its rapid increase and concludes from this that no other changes are needed\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"equates a reduction in the rate at which the magazine\'s circulation is increasing with a decline in the magazine\'s circulation\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"draws a conclusion that simply restates a claim that is presented in support of that conclusion\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"takes a single fact that is incompatible with a general claim as enough to show that claim to be false\\"},{\\"pos\\":5,\\"text\\":\\"treats an occurrence that will ensure a certain outcome as something that is required for that outcome\\"}]},{\\"id\\":168,\\"pt\\":\\"149\\",\\"sec\\":4,\\"pos\\":14,\\"key\\":\\"B\\",\\"questiontext\\":\\"<p style=\\\\\\"display: inline-block; margin-left: 40px; text-indent: -40px;\\\\\\">Columnist: Wildlife activists have proposed that the practice of stringing cable TV lines from the same poles that carry electric power lines should be banned because cable TV lines, while electrically neutral themselves, make it easier for animals to climb near electric power lines, risking electrocution. This particular argument for banning the practice fails, however, since some animals are electrocuted by power lines even where cable TV lines are all underground.</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p>Which one of the following most accurately describes a flaw in the columnist\'s reasoning?</p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"It takes a sufficient condition for an argument\'s being inadequate to be a necessary condition for its being inadequate.\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"It rejects an argument for a proposal merely on the grounds that the proposal would not completely eliminate the problem it is intended to address.\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"It fails to consider the additional advantageous effects that a proposal to address a problem might have.\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"It rejects an argument by criticizing the argument\'s proponents rather than by criticizing its substance.\\"},{\\"pos\\":5,\\"text\\":\\"It rejects a proposal to address a problem merely on the grounds that other proposals to address the problem would also be effective.\\"}]},{\\"id\\":7650,\\"pt\\":\\"102\\",\\"sec\\":4,\\"pos\\":17,\\"key\\":\\"B\\",\\"questiontext\\":\\"<p style=\\\\\\"display: inline-block; margin-left: 40px; text-indent: -40px;\\\\\\">Concerned citizen: The mayor, an outspoken critic of the proposed restoration of city hall, is right when he notes that the building is outdated, but that the restoration would be expensive at a time when the budget is already tight. We cannot afford such a luxury item in this time of financial restraint, he says. However, I respectfully disagree. The building provides the last remaining link to the days of the city’s founding, and preserving a sense of municipal history is crucial to maintaining respect for our city government and its authority. So to the question, “Can we really afford to?” I can only respond, “Can we afford not to?”</p> \\\\r\\\\n<p>Which one of the following most accurately characterizes a flaw in the concerned citizen\'s argument?</p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"The argument is solely an emotional appeal to history.\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"The argument ambiguously uses the word “afford. ”\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"The argument inappropriately appeals to the authority of the mayor.\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"The argument incorrectly presumes that the restoration would be expensive.\\"},{\\"pos\\":5,\\"text\\":\\"The argument inappropriately relies on the emotional connotations of words such as “outdated” and “luxury. ”\\"}]},{\\"id\\":7441,\\"pt\\":\\"105\\",\\"sec\\":4,\\"pos\\":9,\\"key\\":\\"C\\",\\"questiontext\\":\\"<p>To allay public concern about chemicals that are leaking into a river from a chemical company\'s long \xadestablished dump, a company representative said,\\\\r\\\\n\\\\\\"Federal law requires that every new chemical be tested for safety before it is put onto the market. This is analogous to the federal law mandating testing of every pharmaceutical substance for safety.\\\\\\"</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p>Which one of the following, if true, most seriously weakens the representative\'s implied argument that the public need not be concerned about the leak?</p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"When pharmaceutical substances are tested for safety pursuant to federal requirements, a delay is imposed on the entry of potentially lifesaving substances onto the market.\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"Leakage from the dump has occurred in noticeable amounts only in the last few months.\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"Before the federal law requiring testing of nonpharmaceutical chemicals went into effect recently, there were 40,000 such chemicals being manufactured, many of them dangerous.\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"The concentration of chemicals leaking into the river is diluted, first by rainwater and then by the water in the river.\\"},{\\"pos\\":5,\\"text\\":\\"The water in the river is murky because of the runoff of silt from a number of nearby construction projects.\\"}]},{\\"id\\":539,\\"pt\\":\\"144\\",\\"sec\\":2,\\"pos\\":8,\\"key\\":\\"E\\",\\"questiontext\\":\\"<p style=\\\\\\"display: inline-block; margin-left: 40px; text-indent: -40px;\\\\\\">Merton: A study showed that people who live on very busy streets have higher rates of heart disease than average. I conclude that this elevated rate of heart disease is caused by air pollution from automobile exhaust.</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p style=\\\\\\"display: inline-block; margin-left: 40px; text-indent: -40px;\\\\\\">Ortiz: Are you sure? Do we know whether people living on busy streets have other lifestyle factors that are especially conducive to heart disease?</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p>Ortiz criticizes Merton\'s argument by </p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"raising a question about the validity of the study that Merton cites\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"contending that Merton needs to take into account other effects of air pollution\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"claiming that Merton misunderstands a crucial aspect of the study\'s findings\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"raising a counterexample to the general conclusion that Merton draws\\"},{\\"pos\\":5,\\"text\\":\\"suggesting that alternative explanations for the study\'s findings need to be ruled out\\"}]},{\\"id\\":601,\\"pt\\":\\"144\\",\\"sec\\":4,\\"pos\\":21,\\"key\\":\\"C\\",\\"questiontext\\":\\"<p>At a large elementary school researchers studied a small group of children who successfully completed an experimental program in which they learned to play chess. The study found that most of the children who completed the program soon showed a significant increase in achievement levels in all of their schoolwork. Thus, it is likely that the reasoning power and spatial intuition exercised in chess-playing also contribute to achievement in many other areas of intellectual activity.</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p>Which one of the following, if true, most tends to undermine the argument?</p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"Some students who did not participate in the chess program had learned to play chess at home.\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"Those children who began the program but who did not successfully complete it had lower preprogram levels of achievement than did those who eventually did successfully complete the program.\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"Many of the children who completed the program subsequently sought membership on a school chess team that required a high grade average for membership.\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"Some students who did not participate in the chess program participated instead in after-school study sessions that helped them reach much higher levels of achievement in the year after they attended the sessions.\\"},{\\"pos\\":5,\\"text\\":\\"At least some of the students who did not successfully complete the program were nevertheless more talented chess players than some of the students who did complete the program.\\"}]},{\\"id\\":1296,\\"pt\\":\\"145\\",\\"sec\\":4,\\"pos\\":11,\\"key\\":\\"D\\",\\"questiontext\\":\\"<p>Polls have shown that a higher percentage of graduating university students are against proposals to reduce government social services than are students entering their first year at a university. These polls lead us to the conclusion that people with a university education are more likely to favor retaining or increasing the present level of government social services than are members of the overall population.</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p>Which one of the following, if true, most seriously weakens the argument?</p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"The polls of graduating university students were designed to avoid overrepresenting any single academic discipline.\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"The political views of people with a university education are to a large degree influenced by their professors, and university professors are usually against reducing government social services.\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"Polls of retired persons who have not graduated from a university show a higher percentage of persons in favor of reducing government social services than do polls of retired persons who have graduated from a university.\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"Polls of those who graduated from a university more than five years before being polled show a higher percentage of people in favor of reducing government social services than do polls of the overall population.\\"},{\\"pos\\":5,\\"text\\":\\"In the polls cited, graduating university students were more likely to express strong opinions about the question of reducing government social services than were students entering a university.\\"}]},{\\"id\\":1091,\\"pt\\":\\"147\\",\\"sec\\":4,\\"pos\\":7,\\"key\\":\\"D\\",\\"questiontext\\":\\"<p style=\\\\\\"display: inline-block; margin-left: 40px; text-indent: -40px;\\\\\\">Trade negotiator: Increasing economic prosperity in a country tends to bring political freedom to its inhabitants. Therefore, it is wrong for any country to adopt trade policies that are likely to seriously hinder growth in the prosperity of any other country.</p>\\\\r\\\\n<p>Which one of the following principles, if valid, would most help to justify the trade negotiator\'s reasoning?</p>\\",\\"answers\\":[{\\"pos\\":1,\\"text\\":\\"Every country should adopt at least some policies that encourage the development of political freedom in other countries.\\"},{\\"pos\\":2,\\"text\\":\\"Both economic prosperity and political freedom can contribute to the overall well-being of any country\'s inhabitants.\\"},{\\"pos\\":3,\\"text\\":\\"The primary reason that any country seeks economic prosperity is to foster political freedom in that country.\\"},{\\"pos\\":4,\\"text\\":\\"A country should not do anything that might hinder the growth of politic…254152 tokens truncated…onsider that EVEN IF premise is true, STILL POSSIBLE THAT conclusion is wrong\\\\\\".\\\\nThis takes that form. \\\\\\"Even though the reports are incomplete, they may help the cause of making the public better informed about airline safety.\\\\\\"\\\\nThis is making the objection that \\\\\\"something is better than nothing\\\\\\". If the govt didn\'t publish safety stuff, then the public might have no information about airline safety, so even if this leads to incomplete reports, it could be an improvement on before.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"50a5da27-5f6c-4a32-9182-87d26e8fd7f4\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"120eec9e-0836-4745-abad-e86768a2c84b\\", \\"letter\\": \\"B\\", \\"body\\": \\"The author didn\'t need to assume that the public has a right to all info about all matters of public safety. She\'s not even really assuming that the public has a right to this info.\\\\nShe\'s just making a descriptive argument that says, \\\\\\"Hey, govt. I know you\'re trying to boost public knowledge, but I see your action as having a repercussion that would undermine that goal.\\\\\\"\\\\nShe isn\'t evaluating whether it\'s a worthy goal, a rightful goal, etc.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"6dd2899e-674c-4e2b-93fa-0965cf9a8cb4\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"120eec9e-0836-4745-abad-e86768a2c84b\\", \\"letter\\": \\"C\\", \\"body\\": \\"The author doesn\'t need to assume that info about airline safety is impossible to find without government disclosures.\\\\nIt wouldn\'t hurt her argument if there was some possible non-governmental way to obtain a shred of info about airline safety.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"1f4213a7-469a-4f32-9d79-46c75482d0bc\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"120eec9e-0836-4745-abad-e86768a2c84b\\", \\"letter\\": \\"D\\", \\"body\\": \\"There\'s nothing normative in this argument. The author is arguing that a step the govt is taking will undermine what it is hoping to achieve by taking that step.\\\\nOne can make that argument regardless of whether they endorse the government\'s goal, are opposed to it, whether they like the govt or hate it, whether they think the government should report accurate safety info or someone else.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"42363bc9-933c-4248-9923-07484ec224e4\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"95d0bb5b-7cfd-40d7-957b-b0a3609bde89\\", \\"letter\\": \\"A\\", \\"body\\": \\"Even X doesn\'t let me get every single thing I desire. Big whoop. None of us think that there\'s something out there that would let us get every single good we desire, so there\'s it\'s not changing anyone\'s mind about X.\\\\nAlso, this basically goes the opposite direction of what we\'re trying to do: argue that financial rewards are the #1 thing that people care about when choosing a job.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"3729ee1a-6470-4cea-8e97-9a657f2bd4d7\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"95d0bb5b-7cfd-40d7-957b-b0a3609bde89\\", \\"letter\\": \\"B\\", \\"body\\": \\"This establishes that people have a positive attitude towards higher salaries: they\'d rather have it than not have it. But the argument isn\'t about whether you\'d rather have a higher salary than not have it. It\'s about comparing the relative importance of salary to things like coworkers, flexible schedule, a boss you respect, vacation and parental leave policies, etc. This answer doesn\'t address that conversation at all because it\'s comparing two otherwise identical jobs. The only thing different is higher salary, so of course we\'d take the higher salary. There are no trade-offs.\\\\nSay that in selecting a law school, the most important thing to you was school ranking. Financial aid mattered, but not as much. It would be true to say that \\\\\\"you\'d prefer to get lots of financial aid at school X than you would to get a lower amount of financial aid at school X.\\\\\\" That doesn\'t mean financial aid is your #1 concern. It just says you\'d rather have it than not have it, all other things being equal.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"5ac92821-6909-4953-9ae9-b97ff13233f5\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"95d0bb5b-7cfd-40d7-957b-b0a3609bde89\\", \\"letter\\": \\"C\\", \\"body\\": \\"This speaks to the term shift between the surveys about \\\\\\"salary\\\\\\" and the conclusion about \\\\\\"financial rewards\\\\\\".\\\\nIt allows us to argue, \\\\\\"The economists are right that financial rewards are the #1 thing people care about when choosing a job. True, salary is not #1, but the overall compensation package, including health benefits, paid leave, stock options, and salary are the #1 thing people care about.\\\\\\"\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"d83f1649-4c2e-4eef-b0f4-84b643694b2f\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"95d0bb5b-7cfd-40d7-957b-b0a3609bde89\\", \\"letter\\": \\"D\\", \\"body\\": \\"This isn\'t comparative at all, and this whole argument is about comparing the relative importance of things. We don\'t care that people enjoy challenging work. We care about whether they value challenging work more / less than they value financial rewards.\\\\nAlso \\\\\\"many\\\\\\" is a very weak quantity that essentially means \\\\\\"at least 5 or 10\\\\\\", so this isn\'t telling us much about people broadly.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"9a9afa9e-1a51-4bdb-a02b-cc2c45717e67\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"39efdfea-3b62-4e22-9d99-bd97d71204fc\\", \\"letter\\": \\"A\\", \\"body\\": \\"This is far from our predicted missing link. It doesn\'t involve connecting \\\\\\"grades\\\\\\" to \\\\\\"engagement in the learning process\\\\\\".\\\\nWould it hurt this author\'s argument if we negated this and said, \\\\\\"Sometimes schools other than large elementary school are appropriate for study\\\\\\"?\\\\nOf course not. Everyone in the world believes that sometimes it would be appropriate to study schools other than large elementary schools.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"73d09ad7-075f-4caf-937f-57a2edec0503\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"39efdfea-3b62-4e22-9d99-bd97d71204fc\\", \\"letter\\": \\"B\\", \\"body\\": \\"This is an incredibly strong claim that no one in the real world would assent to:\\\\n51% or more of teachers devote an identical amount of personalized attention to every single student in class.\\\\nOur author certainly didn\'t commit to such an extreme idea, and if we negated this and said, \\\\\\"Sorry, author, at most 49% of teachers give identical attention to every student\\\\\\", it wouldn\'t hurt the author in the slightest.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"cf883bae-e16b-47cb-86fd-4fbf63800f57\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"39efdfea-3b62-4e22-9d99-bd97d71204fc\\", \\"letter\\": \\"C\\", \\"body\\": \\"This author never said anything insinuating that reducing class size necessarily brings with it a reduction in the number of teachers.\\\\nThe author is looking at a study where smaller class size went with (presumably) same number of teachers. Because the ratio of students per teacher improved, the teachers could spend more time with individual students.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"f0c578d1-ac6a-4bcb-8286-9508a35af166\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"39efdfea-3b62-4e22-9d99-bd97d71204fc\\", \\"letter\\": \\"D\\", \\"body\\": \\"This is addressing the big gap between the premise, \\\\\\"we reduced class size, but the grades didn\'t change\\\\\\" and the conclusion, \\\\\\"reducing class size doesn\'t lead to students\' becoming more engaged in the learning process\\\\\\".\\\\nThe author must think that \\\\\\"grades change\\\\\\" is connected to \\\\\\"engagement in the learning process\\\\\\".\\\\nIf we negated this answer we would get this objection:\\\\n\\\\\\"yo, author, the degree to which students are engaged in the learning process is not well reflected by grades. So even if grades remained unchanged when that school reduced class sizes, it still may be that engagement in the learning process was changed.\\\\\\"\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"89c594d3-ec0d-45fd-a7b7-531c66cbd51e\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"7ae9a4dc-dc4a-49c4-b7cc-b85e234b189a\\", \\"letter\\": \\"A\\", \\"body\\": \\"She hasn\'t claimed that she\'s already made her money back, or that this water-saving faucet has already paid for itself, so she\'s not committed to assuming that she spent less to install this than what she\'s already saved from it.\\\\nHer argument is just that she\'s been saving money every month, so they didn\'t exaggerate about what she\'d save.\\\\nYou might know that an investment is supposedly going to take several years to pay for itself, but you could still gauge whether it\'s on track to do so by looking at how the first few months go.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"3588dd79-027d-4d03-8143-6eba39c3c90b\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"7ae9a4dc-dc4a-49c4-b7cc-b85e234b189a\\", \\"letter\\": \\"B\\", \\"body\\": \\"If she thinks that the manufacturer\'s didn\'t exaggerate how much she\'d save, then she believes she\'s saving as much as they promised she would.\\\\nIf we negated this assumption it would indeed be an objection: if she hasn\'t saved as much as the manufacturers claimed she would, then they were exaggerating their claims.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"b1a5ca20-99aa-4ee2-950c-71f84777b0bf\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"7ae9a4dc-dc4a-49c4-b7cc-b85e234b189a\\", \\"letter\\": \\"C\\", \\"body\\": \\"Yes, she\'s probably assuming that the manufacturers\' claims are not contradictory (inconsistent = contradictory).\\\\nBut that\'s not a great objection. She said, \\\\\\"Since I\'m saving money each month, they must have been right about how much I\'d save.\\\\\\"\\\\nIf we said, \\\\\\"Oh, yeah? We\'ll you\'re assuming that the manufacturers\' claims don\'t contradict each other\\\\\\" we would sound kind of crazy. This doesn\'t relate to the author\'s bad reasoning move from \\\\\\"saving money\\\\\\" to \\\\\\"saving as much as they promised\\\\\\".\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"f53189c7-bd15-464b-8fc9-a45e77d86a4f\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"7ae9a4dc-dc4a-49c4-b7cc-b85e234b189a\\", \\"letter\\": \\"D\\", \\"body\\": \\"The truth value of the author\'s conclusion hinges only on whether she\'s saving as much water as the manufacturers claimed. So whether or not customers are satisfied with the volume of water is out of scope.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"603fb4a4-529e-40e8-9c61-ecce3eae2bfe\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"61c29b65-8542-49a5-b1a4-8a438ef4e432\\", \\"letter\\": \\"B\\", \\"body\\": \\"The conclusion is about which bank is more costly to bank at.\\\\nThe evidence is about one of the costs involved in banking at each bank. So the evidence is definitely relevant to the issue under discussion.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"47c4d36e-cd23-4f02-96ce-a5909fa62e96\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"85c02cf5-cdc1-44b7-a7df-4ca110539497\\", \\"letter\\": \\"A\\", \\"body\\": \\"That\'s fine, but it doesn\'t change the fact that those old cars are still 30% of the local air pollution. If anything, the fact that these old cars are only 1% mean that it\'s more feasible for us to buy them all up (which would strengthen).\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"56f25df3-ccc8-4ab1-ad3b-c5de884e63a5\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"85c02cf5-cdc1-44b7-a7df-4ca110539497\\", \\"letter\\": \\"B\\", \\"body\\": \\"We don\'t care about money at all. We\'re only measuring the impact on air pollution, but this answer should be very un-tempting since it argues in favor of doing the car buying idea.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"71dd5f4c-e393-47f7-85d0-dbf9448cc7f3\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"85c02cf5-cdc1-44b7-a7df-4ca110539497\\", \\"letter\\": \\"C\\", \\"body\\": \\"This answer is saying that the people who seem willing to take part in this program are almost always people with an old car they don\'t drive (you know that neighbor with an old car in the garage or up on blocks). Buying those cars is irrelevant to air pollution, because those old cars weren\'t on the road.\\\\nWe WANT to be buying the old cars that people are still driving around, spewing noxious exhaust everywhere. But \\\\\\"almost none\\\\\\" of those people are selling us their cars (presumably because they need those cars as transportation, and company is offering so little money in this buyback program that someone who sold their old car wouldn\'t get enough money to then buy a newer one)\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"6b598518-7d1e-4f77-a089-db63e615fec6\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"85c02cf5-cdc1-44b7-a7df-4ca110539497\\", \\"letter\\": \\"D\\", \\"body\\": \\"Cool fact, choice (D). Unfortunately, this has nothing to do with comparing the two options that are relevant to the conclusion: redesigning plants vs. buying pre-1980 cars.\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"18f66939-7d3e-4b23-98b3-10ae440e7c41\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"a315d220-b4f0-4b45-aff9-1119c32e81f4\\", \\"letter\\": \\"A\\", \\"body\\": \\"Raise grades → More study time\\\\nMore study time → Good time\\\\nRaise grades. management\\\\n⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯\\\\nGood time management\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"f31ef25c-6951-4d02-b607-aa73bf9d2673\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"a315d220-b4f0-4b45-aff9-1119c32e81f4\\", \\"letter\\": \\"B\\", \\"body\\": \\"We only have 2 premises (original had 3), so that\'s a quick way to get rid of this. We don\'t have two conditional premises; we have one conditional and one MOST claim. The conclusion also brings in a brand new idea (bug-eating insects).\\\\ncarbs from others → make own\\\\nmost plants → carbs from bugs\\\\n⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯⎯\\\\nBug-eating plants → Photosynthesis\\", \\"created_at\\": \\"2026-08-03T02:43:58.177541+00:00\\"}, {\\"id\\": \\"6267c332-f58e-4748-afe8-c6b456ca7199\\", \\"explanation_id\\": \\"a315d220-b4f0-4b45-aff9-1119c32e81f4\\", \\"letter\\": \\"C\\", \\"body\\": \\"We only have 1 premise (original had 3), so that\'s a quick way to get rid of this. We don\'t have two conditional premises; we have one conditional.
